During the [2021 Peruvian general election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Peruvian_general_election), [Keiko Fujimori](4643445.md)'s campaign was criticized for being [antidemocratic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Criticism_of_democracy) and launching a [smear campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smear_campaign) against eventual winner [Pedro Castillo](49637220.md), using [racism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racism) and the [terruqueo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terruqueo) to damage his reputation.[^cite_note-Public_Radio_International-229][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAsensioCamachoGonzálezGrompone202127–71-24][^cite_note-The_Guardian-2021-25][^cite_note-RPP-2019-137][^cite_note-The_Washington_Post-247] Following Castillo's victory against her, Fujimori and her allies would attempt to overturn the election by making unverified claims of [electoral fraud](382165.md).[^cite_note-Collyns-2021-237][^cite_note-Swissinfo-2021-238][^cite_note-France_24-2021-239] Fujimori also came under controversy after it was revealed that former [SIN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Intelligence_Service_\(Peru\)) leader [Vladimiro Montesinos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vladimiro_Montesinos), who is imprisoned, was involved in the campaign to overturn the election.[^cite_note-gestion.pe-273] On 19 July, following Fujimori's concession, the [National Office of Electoral Processes](5446067.md) confirmed Castillo's victory. However, Castillo's presidency was marred with [corruption scandals](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corrupci%C3%B3n%20en%20el%20gobierno%20de%20Pedro%20Castillo) and several probes were launched against him. Congress also heavily obstructed much of his actions, specifically done by the [far-right](221220.md) bloc led by [Popular Force](15247524.md), [Popular Renewal](66197743.md), and [Advance Country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Advance_Country). Castillo's actions were also seen as undemocratic following [protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Peruvian_economic_protests) against his administration.[^cite_note-437][^cite_note-438] His undemocratic actions continued during his [third impeachment process](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third_impeachment_and_removal_of_Pedro_Castillo), in which he [executed a self-coup](72438093.md), which was criticized domestically and internationally as antidemocratic and authoritarian, which led to his removal.

Dina Boluarte's presidency was seen as authoritarian following heavy repression of unrest.[^cite_note-AUTHOR-18] [Fujimorists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fujimorism) began to pack government institutions through sham nominations into the [Constitutional Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_Court_of_Peru) and the [Ombudsman office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peruvian_ombudsman). Other governmental institutions like the [Supreme Court](8801058.md) also showed bias towards the Boluarte administration, specifically after the ban on protests in May 2023.[^cite_note-439]

#### Media censorship

Main article: [Mass media in Peru](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_media_in_Peru)

During the 2021 general election, major media outlets were accused of outright support for [Keiko Fujimori](4643445.md)'s campaign and her fear mongering campaign against Pedro Castillo.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAsensioCamachoGonzálezGrompone202164–65-440][^cite_note-441][^cite_note-442]

The National Association of Journalists indicated that 21 journalists were victims of aggression between 7 and 11 December.[^cite_note-443] Journalists interviewed by [Wayka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wayka) reported that authorities would frequently attack press workers and would attempt to prevent photographers from capturing images of individuals being detained. Similar incidents of authorities preventing journalists from documenting the protests were collected by [OjoPúblico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OjoP%C3%BAblico). One photojournalist for the EFE, Aldair Mejía reported that he was threatened by police during a protest in [Juliaca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juliaca), saying that an officer told him "I'll blow off your head and you get out of here dead"; Mejía was later shot in the leg by police while covering protests.

The media in Peru was also criticized for overlooking the massacres at [Ayacucho](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayacucho_massacre) and [Juliaca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juliaca_massacre).[^cite_note-444][^cite_note-445][^cite_note-446]

| External videos |
| --- |
| video icon [A video showing police shooting a protester in the head with a tear gas canister, medical volunteers providing aid and the testimony of one of the responders](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=n2ZOSKqhnck) on [YouTube](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/YouTube_video_\(identifier\)) |

Following the death of a protester on 28 January 2023, journalist Carlos Cornejo of the state-owned [TV Perú](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TV_Per%C3%BA) reported to audiences, stating "It's good news that the roads have been opened. Some bad news: the murder of a citizen at the hands of the police. ... They will want to tell us that it was a stone, they will want to tell us that it was not them, they will want to say anything, but the images do not lie: we all saw that the police killed Victor Santisteban".[^cite_note-La_Republica-2023g-447] The government would attempt to deny that police killed the protester. Despite two videos showing the protester being shot in the head with a tear gas canister, General Victor Zanabria of the Peruvian National Police said it was "not yet determined" what killed the protester.[^cite_note-Saavedra-2023-448] Following Cornejo's report on the death of the protester, the National Institute of Radio and Television of Peru (IRTP), the parent company of TV Perú, refused to renew the reporter's contract on 31 January.[^cite_note-La_Republica-2023g-447][^cite_note-La_Republica-2023h-449] According to the National Association of Journalists of Peru (ANP), the actions against Cornejo were "an indicted and arbitrary dismissal", stating "In circumstances in which, from all media, and essentially state media, it is imperative to guarantee a plurality of approaches and voices to promote public debate. Extinguishing critical positions can even constitute censorship".[^cite_note-La_Republica-2023h-449]

#### Violence and Human Rights concerns

Further information: [Peruvian protests (2022–2023) § State violence towards protesters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peruvian_protests_\(2022%E2%80%932023\)#State_violence_towards_protesters), and [2020 Peruvian protests § Protest violence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Peruvian_protests#Protest_violence)

See also: [La Resistencia Dios, Patria y Familia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Resistencia_Dios,_Patria_y_Familia)

| video icon [Authorities shooting a protester in the head with a tear gas canister](https://twitter.com/AlertaNews24/status/1602610398712111104) |
| --- |

Violence in Peru during the political crisis has risen, specifically during the presidencies of [Pedro Castillo](49637220.md) and [Dina Boluarte](67870874.md) as their respective governments battled unrest against them.[^cite_note-dw-2022-368]

The [Peruvian government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Peru) has had a history of [human rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_right) abuses during [Operation Condor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Condor) and the [internal Conflict in Peru](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internal_Conflict_in_Peru), led mostly by [Francisco Morales Bermúdez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francisco_Morales_Berm%C3%BAdez) and [Alberto Fujimori](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alberto_Fujimori), respectively. Within those periods, [fear mongering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fear_mongering) in the media was high, as were [enforced disappearances](686148.md), [extrajudicial executions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extrajudicial_killing), and [massacres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massacre). The [Peruvian Armed Forces](23438.md) have a history of [impunity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Impunity); between 2003 and 2020, the armed forces were responsible for 167 deaths, while the perpetrators received little to no consequences for their actions.[^cite_note-infobae-2022-47][^cite_note-450][^cite_note-ojo-publico-2022-376] The government first participated in protest violence during the [2020 Peruvian protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Peruvian_protests), in which 2 deaths were reported.[^cite_note-451][^cite_note-452][^cite_note-453] Reports of [police brutality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Police_brutality) and [political repression](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_repression) were widely reported to the media, and ultimately stopped after [President](239109.md) [Manuel Merino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manuel_Merino) resigned. Reports of violence came to light again during the [2021 general election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Peruvian_general_election), in which members of the [neofascist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neo-fascism) [La Resistencia Dios, Patria y Familia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Resistencia_Dios,_Patria_y_Familia) clashed with members of the [far-left](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Far-left_politics) [Free Peru](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_Peru). La Resistencia also attempted a [January 6](66307490.md)-type attack on the [Government Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_Palace,_Peru), which was repelled by police.[^cite_note-elmundo.es-259][^cite_note-454][^cite_note-'La_Pestilencia'_por_dentro-15][^cite_note-455] Following the victory of [Pedro Castillo](49637220.md) as president and his assumption to the post, La Resistancia advocated for his overthrow and participated in attacks against his government, aligning themselves with the far-right [Congress](1571007.md).[^cite_note-456][^cite_note-larepublica.pe-2021-300] The group also attacked journalists.[^cite_note-457]

Following Castillo's removal in December 2022 and the beginning of protests, the government of [Dina Boluarte](67870874.md) participated in human rights abuses, with her forces allegedly using [torture](47702.md), [unauthorized intrusions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Police_raid),[^cite_note-458][^cite_note-459] police brutality, political repression, extrajudicial killings,[^cite_note-460][^cite_note-461] and massacres. The abuses also allegedly constituted [racism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racism), disproportionately affecting impoverished indigenous communities.[^cite_note-462][^cite_note-463] The participation of La Resistencia in violence against protesters also brought more controversy on top of the government repression.[^cite_note-464][^cite_note-465]

[^cite_note-163]: &#x20;Peru has two Vice Presidents, a First and Second Vice President. The most recent holders of the office of First and Second Vice President were Vizcarra and [Mercedes Aráoz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercedes_Ar%C3%A1oz), respectively, who were elected in the 2016 election.<sup>[\[157\]](#cite_note-araoz-157)</sup><sup>[\[158\]](#cite_note-vizcarra-158)</sup> Vizcarra left the office of First Vice President vacant after he succeeded Kuzcynski as President, leaving Aráoz as the sole Vice President,<sup>[\[157\]](#cite_note-araoz-157)</sup> while Aráoz resigned as Second Vice President on 1 October 2019 after the [Congress of Peru](1571007.md) named Aráoz acting President the day before after having declared Vizacarra temporarily unfit for office,<sup>[\[157\]](#cite_note-araoz-157)</sup><sup>[\[158\]](#cite_note-vizcarra-158)</sup><sup>[\[159\]](#cite_note-dissolution-159)</sup> despite Congress having itself been dissolved earlier that day by President Vizcarra, resulting in a [constitutional crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Peruvian_constitutional_crisis).<sup>[\[157\]](#cite_note-araoz-157)</sup><sup>[\[158\]](#cite_note-vizcarra-158)</sup><sup>[\[159\]](#cite_note-dissolution-159)</sup><sup>[\[160\]](#cite_note-araoz2-160)</sup><sup>[\[161\]](#cite_note-araoz3-161)</sup> However, her resignation was not official until it was accepted on 7 May 2020 by the new Congress of Peru sworn on 16 March 2020, since in Peru the resignation of the vice president has to be accepted by Congress and that institution was not celebrating meetings given its dissolution.<sup>[\[162\]](#cite_note-162)</sup>
